The time of a lookup could depend on the bytes of a valid key. Now Bolt finds the valid key by a SipHash with a random key, and then compares the two keys in constant time. A client cannot compute the hashes, so the time of a check does not show the bytes of a key.
- **A `Header()` struct error names the header** - A missing field of a `Header()` struct gave a 422 with a string `detail`. A field that failed in Python, for example a `msgspec.Meta` constraint or a `Literal`, gave `loc` `["body", ...]` with the encoded field name. Now each such field gives one error in the `detail` list, with `loc` `["header", "x-count"]`, as a struct with `Query()` does. One response holds all such fields. Each error has `input`, the value of the header, as for a scalar `Header()`. A value that Rust cannot convert, such as `abc` for an `int`, still gives a string `detail`. A missing field now raises `RequestValidationError` in place of `HTTPException`. Thus an `error_formatter` gets the `errors` list, and a Python middleware that catches `HTTPException` does not get the error. For these errors, `WebSocketTestClient` now raises `RequestValidationError`.
- **A compressed response has one `Vary: accept-encoding`** - The compression middleware added `Vary: accept-encoding`, and the encoder of Actix added it again. Thus each compressed response sent the header twice. Now only the encoder adds it, when it compresses the body. A `Vary` of the handler, such as `Vary: Origin`, stays first.
- **`ErrorHandlerMiddleware` keeps a validation error a 422** - The middleware turned each exception other than `HTTPException` into a 500. Thus a missing field of a struct parameter or a JSON body that failed validation gave a 500. Now a `ValidationException` or a `msgspec.ValidationError` passes through the middleware, and Bolt answers 422.
- **A failed JWKS refresh is logged** - Bolt keeps the last keys when a JWKS refresh fails. It did not log the failure. After a key rotation, each token from the new key got a 401, and nothing showed that the provider failed. Now each failure logs a WARNING to the `django_bolt.auth` logger: an HTTP error, a document without a `keys` array, a document with no usable key, and an error in the refresh callback.
- **`api.mount("/", app)` serves the routes of the mounted API** - A mount path of only slashes gave each route a path with two slashes, such as `//users`. No request matched these routes, and the client got 404. Now `"/"` mounts at the root, as `""` does.
- **Bolt reads the last `X-Real-IP` line of a trusted proxy** - Bolt read the first `X-Real-IP` line. A proxy that adds its own line keeps the line of the client first. Thus a client behind such a proxy could pick `REMOTE_ADDR` and its `@rate_limit(key="ip")` bucket. Now Bolt reads the last line, as for `X-Forwarded-Proto`. This applies only when the proxy sends no `X-Forwarded-For`. A proxy that replaces the header, as the nginx example does, was not affected.
- **The lifespan of a mounted API runs** - `api.mount()` copied the routes of the mounted API and ignored its `lifespan`. The startup code of the mounted API never ran, so its routes worked without their resources. Now `runbolt`, `TestClient` and `AsyncTestClient` run the lifespan of each mounted API. Startup runs the parent first, then the mounted APIs in mount order. Shutdown runs in reverse order.
- **`@rate_limit(key="ip")` counts an IPv6 client per /64 network** - Bolt counted each IPv6 address on its own. A provider usually gives one client a /64 network or more. Thus a client could send each request from a new address and never reach the limit. Now Bolt counts an IPv6 client per /64 network. The address fallback of `key="user"` and `key="api_key"` does the same. The new setting `BOLT_RATE_LIMIT_IPV6_PREFIX` sets the prefix length, from 0 to 128. Set 128 to count each address, as before. An IPv4 client is still counted per address. The 429 log line names the network, and `REMOTE_ADDR` keeps the full address.
- **Django middleware keeps the Content-Type of a response** - A route with Django middleware sent `application/json` for an `HTML`, `PlainText`, `bytes` or `str` response. A `Response` with a `media_type` and no headers did too. These responses keep their content type in the response type, and the middleware bridge did not read it. Now the bridge sends the content type of the response type. A 204 with no body and a `Redirect` now send no Content-Type, as they do without Django middleware. A Django response without a Content-Type, such as the 304 of `ConditionalGetMiddleware`, also stays without one. A `MiddlewareResponse` with a response type that Rust does not know gets `application/octet-stream`, as Rust gives it.
- **The lifespan examples read the state from the API object** - The examples in the Lifespan and Testing pages read `request.app`. A request has no `app` attribute, so each example answered 500. The examples now read the module-level `api` object that the lifespan gets as `app`.
- **Django middleware keeps a bytes-like body** - On a route with Django middleware, a `bytearray` or `memoryview` body that a Python middleware set went out as its repr. A `memoryview` thus sent a process address, such as `<memory at 0x7f...>`. Now the bridge sends the bytes of the body.
- **`CursorPagination` does not give `has_next` with no cursor** - When the last row of a page had a null ordering value, the page said `has_next: true` with `next_cursor: null`. A client could not get the next page. The same happened when a `values()` queryset did not include the ordering field. Now the paginator raises `ValueError` in both cases, with a message that names the field, as it does for a `values()` queryset without the primary key.
- **`CsrfViewMiddleware` reads the token of a multipart form** - The bridge parsed `request.POST` for `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` bodies only. For a multipart form, Django middleware saw an empty `request.POST` and `request.FILES`. Thus `CsrfViewMiddleware` rejected each multipart form with a valid `csrfmiddlewaretoken` field. It also parsed a urlencoded body for every method and ignored the charset. Now the Django request has `content_type` and `content_params`, and it reads `request.POST` and `request.FILES` on first use, as `WSGIRequest` does. An unreadable form, for example a multipart body with no boundary, gives a 400 response when a middleware reads it.
- **A response validation error has the location `response`** - A response validation error that msgspec raised had `body` as the first item of its `loc`. That is the location of the request body. Now it has `response`, as the other response validation errors have. An `error_formatter` gets the new `loc`.
- **Python middleware reads the Content-Type of a built-in response** - For an `HTML`, `PlainText`, `bytes`, `str` or JSON response, `response.headers` of a Python middleware had no Content-Type, also with custom headers or cookies. Now it has the value that the client gets. The header names of `response.headers` ignore letter case, and Bolt stores each name in lower case. Thus `headers["Content-Type"]` reads and replaces the value of `content-type`, and the last write wins. A Content-Type that middleware deletes, or sets to an empty value, is not sent. Each response through Python middleware now takes about 300 ns more in Python.
- **A `list[Struct]` response converts an object that follows a dict** - The fast path of a `list[Struct]` response checked only the first element of the list. When the list started with a dict and held an object later, such as a model instance, the response was a 500. A handler that takes only `request` uses another path, and gave a 200 for the same list. Now the fast path sends such a list to that path, which converts the object by attribute. An invalid element still gives the same 500 and the same error. The fast path also sends each other error to the slow path, such as an untagged union of Structs or an error in `__post_init__`. Thus the response is a response validation error with its message, not a bare 500.
- **An async serializer route loads a relation that the field type does not show** - In an async route, Bolt loads an unloaded relation of a serializer with `afrom_model()`. It skipped this step when no field was nested, had a `source`, or had a collection type. A field such as `profile: dict | None` can name a relation, so such a route gave a 500. Now an async route always loads an unloaded relation. When no relation needs a load, the route does no more work than before.
- **`request.path` in Django middleware is the decoded path** - The bridge gave Django middleware the percent-encoded path. Thus `get_full_path()` and `build_absolute_uri()` encoded it twice (`/p/%25C3%25A9`), and a redirect from `CommonMiddleware` had a wrong `Location`. Now `path`, `path_info` and `META["PATH_INFO"]` hold the decoded path, as Django servers give it. `%2F` becomes `/`. An invalid UTF-8 sequence stays percent-encoded, as in the WSGI handler of Django.
- **The Rust core writes its log records** - The Rust crates of Bolt log with the `log` crate, but Bolt installed no backend. Thus each record was lost, also errors such as an exception in a mounted ASGI app or in an MCP dispatch. Now each record is one line on stderr, for example `[django-bolt] ERROR bolt_asgi::asgi_http: ...`. The effective level of the `django_bolt` logger sets the level. Bolt reads it once, when `runbolt` starts the server or a test client starts. Without a `LOGGING` setting, the level is `DEBUG` when `DEBUG = True` and `WARNING` otherwise. Other crates, such as Actix, write only their `WARNING` and `ERROR` records. A line break in a message is escaped, so each record stays on one line. A record below the level costs one atomic load in Rust and does not go through Python.
- **Each 401 has the `WWW-Authenticate` header** - The 401 of Rust for a missing or invalid credential has `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. The 401 of `CurrentUser` and the 401 of a revoked token had no such header. Now they have the header that Rust sends for the route. A route with no auth backend has no challenge.
- **A Bolt response behind Django middleware keeps its content type and headers** - A Python middleware can return a Bolt response object, such as `HTML`, `PlainText`, `Redirect` or `Response(..., media_type=...)`. When Django middleware ran around it, the bridge sent `application/json` and dropped the headers. Thus an `HTML` response had the wrong content type, a `Redirect` had no `Location` header, and a `StreamingResponse` sent the repr of its generator. Now the bridge serializes the object as a handler return does. The client gets the status, the content type, the body, every header and the cookies of the object. The path with no Django middleware is not changed.
- **A 500 from the Rust core is valid JSON for each message** - An exception that `_dispatch` does not catch, or an exception from an ASGI mount, gets a 500 from the Rust core. With `DEBUG=True`, the body holds the message and the traceback. The core escaped only `"`, `\`, newline, carriage return and tab. Another control character in the message, such as an ANSI color code, gave a body that was not valid JSON. Now the core escapes each control character.
- **The `Bearer` scheme of a credential has no case** - Bolt removed the scheme from a token only when it was exactly `Bearer `. Thus `Authorization: bearer <token>` got a 401, although RFC 9110 says that the name of an auth scheme is case-insensitive. Now Bolt compares the scheme without case, for a JWT and for the `Bearer` or `ApiKey` scheme before an API key. The comparison does not allocate.
- **A marked sync wrapper can return a coroutine that awaits** - Bolt runs an async handler with no `await` in its code without an asyncio task. To find such a handler, Bolt read only the opcodes of the function. A sync function that `inspect.markcoroutinefunction` marks has no `await` in its code, but its coroutine can await. Such a handler gave a 500 on each request: "Handler awaited unexpectedly in sync dispatch". Now Bolt runs only an `async def` with no `await` without a task. A marked sync function, such as a decorator wrapper, runs in a task.
- **`request.user` of a token with no user answers 401** - A valid token can name a user that does not exist, for example a deleted user. Then `request.user.id` raised `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'id'`, and the client got a 500. `CurrentUser` gave a 401 for the same request. Now the read of an attribute of such a `request.user` raises `Unauthorized`, and the client gets a 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. Bolt still does not load the user before the first read. Thus `request.user` is a falsy object there and not `None`, and `await request.auser()` gives `None`. The docstring of `Request.user` said `None`. The authentication guide said that `request.user` is `AnonymousUser` for a request with no valid token. It is `None`, and `await request.auser()` gives `AnonymousUser`.
- **A bare return keeps an explicit `status_code` that a `response_model` dict does not list** - With `status_code=201` and `response_model={200: Item, ...: Error}`, a bare dict or list return got status 200. Bolt used the first entry of the dict. Now the catch-all entry serves the return, with the status of the route. Without `...` in the dict, the catch-all type is `Any`. Add the status to the dict to keep a type for it, for example to select the fields of a QuerySet.
- **A session guard rejection uses the error format of the API** - On a route with `SessionAuthentication`, Python checks the guards of a request with a session cookie. Its 401 and 403 did not pass through the `error_formatter` of the API, and the 401 had no `WWW-Authenticate` header. Thus a stale cookie got `{"detail":"Authentication required"}` as `application/json`, while a request with no cookie got the formatted error with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. Now the two rejections are the same response, and a session 403 is the same response as a token 403. The redirect of `login_url` does not change.
- **A bare return on an unlisted status uses the fields of the `...` type** - With `status_code=200` and `response_model={404: Err, ...: list[Row]}`, the `...` entry serves a bare return. Registration did not read the fields of the `...` type. Thus a bare QuerySet or a list of model instances gave a 500 with `TypeError: Unsupported type`. Now the `...` entry selects the fields of `Row`, as a listed status does.
- **`APIKeyAuthentication` rejects an unsafe key configuration** - `APIKeyAuthentication(api_keys={""})` accepted a request with an empty `X-API-Key` header. A missing environment variable in `api_keys={os.environ.get("API_KEY", "")}` caused this. A str, such as `api_keys="sk-prod-123abc"`, made each character a valid key, and the real key got a 401. A str value in `key_permissions` made each character a permission. Now the constructor raises `ImproperlyConfigured` for these values. `api_keys` must be a set, list or tuple of non-empty strings. Each value of `key_permissions` must be a set, list or tuple of strings. A key of `key_permissions` that is not in `api_keys` also raises, because that key cannot authenticate.
- **A `(status, list)` tuple uses the fields of its entry** - With `response_model={200: list[Row], ...: list[Row]}`, `return 201, [article]` sent the list to the JSON encoder as it was. Thus a list of model instances gave a 500 with `TypeError: Unsupported type`. Now the tuple uses the fields of `Row`, as a bare return does.
- **`CursorPagination` finds the primary key of a `values()` row by each of its names** - With `ordering = "-pk"`, a handler that returned `Article.objects.values("id", "title")` got a 500 with `ValueError: CursorPagination ordering '-pk' needs 'pk' in values() to make the next cursor`. The row had the primary key as `id`. An ordering of `"-id"` with `values("pk", "title")` had the same problem. Now an ordering by the primary key reads the value from the row as the column name or as `pk`. The tie-breaker did this already.
- **`TestClient` applies `BOLT_ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS`** - Only `runbolt` read the setting, at startup. Under `TestClient`, a `FileResponse` served each file, and a test could pass where the server answers 403. Now the first `FileResponse` reads the setting if nothing read it. `override_settings` and the `settings` fixture of pytest-django change the limit for the next `FileResponse`. `runbolt` still reads the setting at startup, so a wrong value stops the server.
- **A dependency object with `async def __call__` is awaited** - `Depends(obj)` for an object whose class has `async def __call__` did not await the call. The handler got a coroutine object, and Python gave `RuntimeWarning: coroutine ... was never awaited`. Bolt now finds this shape at registration and awaits the call, for an async and for a sync handler.
- **`format_sse_event` frames bytes data with a line break** - `format_sse_event(data_bytes=b"a\nb")` with no other field gave `data: a\nb`. The second line had no `data:` prefix, so a client dropped it. `data_str` and bytes data with an `event`, `id` or `retry` gave one `data:` line for each line. Now bytes data alone does the same. The SSE framing of a yielded object does not change: its JSON bytes have no line break.
- **A revoked token stays revoked until `JWTAuthentication` stops accepting it** - `JWTAuthentication` accepts a token until `exp + leeway`, 60 seconds after `exp` by default. `DjangoCacheRevocation` and `DjangoORMRevocation` kept a revocation entry only until `exp`. So the backend accepted a logged-out token again for the last `leeway` seconds. A token after its `exp` but inside the leeway could not be revoked at all. Now the two stores have a `leeway` argument, 60 seconds by default, and keep an entry until `exp + leeway`. If you set a larger `leeway` on `JWTAuthentication`, pass the same value to the store. `JWTAuthentication` raises `ImproperlyConfigured` when the `leeway` of its `revocation_store` is smaller than its own. A store with no `leeway` attribute, such as `InMemoryRevocation` or a store of your own, is not checked.
- **The logout routes reject a refresh token with no family** - `/auth/refresh` of `JWTAuthViews` rejects a refresh token with no `fam` claim. The logout routes did not check this claim. Thus `/auth/logout` gave a 500 for such a token, and `/auth/logout-all` logged out each session of the user. Now the two routes answer 401 for this token, as `/auth/refresh` does.
- **`@paginate` finds the request of each handler** - `@paginate` looked only for a parameter with the name `request`. A handler with no request parameter gave a 500 response to each request, although the docstrings show that form. A handler with the request under the name `req` after another parameter also gave a 500 response, because `@paginate` took the other argument as the request. Now `@paginate` uses the rule of the framework: the request parameter has the name `request` or `req`. For a handler with no request parameter, `@paginate` adds one to the signature that the framework reads. It does not pass that argument to the handler.
- **`CursorPagination` names the QuerySet that it needs** - A handler with `@paginate(CursorPagination)` that returned a list got a 500 with `AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'model'`. The `paginate` docstring said that each scheme accepts a list. A cursor is a filter on the database, so `CursorPagination` cannot use a list. Now it raises `TypeError` with the reason, and the message names `PageNumberPagination` and `LimitOffsetPagination` for a list. The docstrings and the pagination page say this.
- **`max_page_size` limits the default page size** - A pagination class that set only `max_page_size` kept the default `page_size` of 100. Thus `class P(PageNumberPagination): max_page_size = 2` gave a page of 100 rows, and `LimitOffsetPagination` gave `limit: 100`. `CursorPagination` had the same problem. The limit applied only to a size from the query string. Now the default size is the smaller of `page_size` and `max_page_size`.
- **A Django middleware can answer with a streaming or a file response** - Django middleware that returned a `StreamingHttpResponse` or a `FileResponse` gave a 500 response. The bridge read `response.content`, and a streaming response has none. Now the bridge sends the chunks of `streaming_content`, sync or async, and closes the Django response at the end. It drops the `Content-Length` header, because the stream has no known length.
- **An `Enum` or `Literal` parameter converts and validates its value** - A path, query, header, cookie or form parameter with an `Enum` or `Literal` type got the text from the request, and Bolt accepted each value. Thus a handler that read `.name` or `.value` raised an error, although the OpenAPI schema showed the allowed values. Now the handler gets the `Enum` member or the `Literal` value. Another value gives a 422 that names the parameter. A `list[Enum]` parameter and a field of a parameter model converted before this change.
- **A paginated handler can return a `range` or other sequence** - With `PageNumberPagination` or `LimitOffsetPagination`, a handler that returned `range(7)` or a `collections.UserList` got a 500 with `TypeError: range.count() takes exactly one argument (0 given)`. The paginator called `count()` to get the total, and the `count(value)` of a sequence counts one value. Now the total of a sequence is its `len()`. A list and a tuple had no problem.
- **`request.GET` in Django middleware keeps every value of a repeated key** - The bridge built `request.GET` from the query dict of Bolt. That dict holds one value for a key, unless the handler declares the parameter as a list. Thus `?tag=a&tag=b` gave `request.GET.getlist("tag")` equal to `["b"]` for a handler with no `tag` parameter. Now the bridge parses the encoded query string, as Django does. Django also raises a 400 response for a query string with more than `DATA_UPLOAD_MAX_NUMBER_FIELDS` parameters.
- **`iter_lines()` and `iter_content()` of the test client read multi-byte text** - A streamed test response read its body in chunks of 8192 bytes for `iter_lines()`, or `chunk_size` bytes for `iter_content(decode_unicode=True)`, and decoded each chunk alone. A chunk that ended inside a UTF-8 character raised `UnicodeDecodeError`. Now `iter_lines()` splits the bytes first, and `iter_content()` uses an incremental decoder. `iter_lines(decode_unicode=False)` now gives `bytes` lines. Before, it gave `str` lines.
- **`CursorPagination` orders by a ForeignKey or by a deferred field** - With `ordering = "author"` on a model whose `author` is a ForeignKey, a page with a next page got a 500. To make the cursor, the paginator read the related object. That ran a query on the event loop (`SynchronousOnlyOperation`), or, with `select_related`, gave an object that JSON cannot hold (`TypeError: Unsupported type`). A queryset that deferred the ordering field (`defer()`, `only()`) got the same `SynchronousOnlyOperation`. Now the cursor of a ForeignKey holds the key, and the paginator loads a deferred field on the ORM executor. If the related model has `Meta.ordering`, Django does not order the ForeignKey name by the key. Then the paginator raises `ValueError`, and the message gives the key name to use, for example `author_id`.
- **A `list[Struct]` parameter with no marker reads the JSON body** - Bolt read a parameter such as `users: list[CreateUser]` from the query string. A query string cannot hold a list of structs, so each request got 422 with `Missing required query parameter`. Now a list, tuple or set of `msgspec.Struct` or dataclass items is the JSON body, as one struct is. A `GET`, `HEAD`, `DELETE` or `OPTIONS` route with such a parameter now raises `TypeError` at registration, as it does for one struct.
- **A cursor with an integer that the database cannot hold gives the first page** - With an ordering that needs the tie-breaker, for example `ordering = "author"`, a cursor that held a primary key of `18446744073709551615` got a 500. On SQLite the error was `OverflowError: Python int too large to convert to SQLite INTEGER`. An integer ordering value had the same problem. A client can make such a cursor. Now `CursorPagination` compares each integer of the cursor with the integer range of its column in the database of the QuerySet. A ForeignKey has the range of its target. A value out of the range means no cursor, as a cursor that does not decode.
- **A cached dependency runs one time when async dependencies run at the same time** - Two or more async dependencies of a handler run at the same time. When two of them needed the same dependency, and that dependency had an `await`, each one found an empty cache and called it. Thus `a=Depends(dep), b=Depends(dep)` called `dep` two times and gave two values. Now a cached dependency that two of them need runs first, and each one reads its value from the cache of the request.
- **A response that Rust cannot build gives a 500 that does not tell the cause to the client** - A handler can return a response with a status that is not valid, for example `JSON(data, status_code=70000)`. Rust finds this error when it builds the response. For an async handler, the body of the 500 told the cause, also with `DEBUG = False`. For a sync handler, the body was `Internal Server Error`, but no log record told the cause. Now both handlers get the 500 of each other exception: the body tells the cause only with `DEBUG = True`. Bolt writes the cause to stderr as an ERROR record of the Rust core.
- **`NotFound("User not found")` is a 404 with that detail** - The first positional argument of `HTTPException` and its subclasses is `status_code`. Thus `NotFound("User not found")` raised `ValueError: 'User not found' is not a valid HTTPStatus` in the handler, and the client got a 500. Now a `str` in the first position is the detail, and the status is the status of the class. A second detail with it raises `TypeError`. Also, a status with no standard phrase and no detail, such as `HTTPException(499)`, raised the same `ValueError`. Now its detail is `Client Error` (4xx) or `Server Error` (5xx).
- **A session `permissions` guard needs `get_all_permissions` of the user model** - For a `Requires("permissions", ...)` guard on a session route, startup checked that the user model has `aget_all_permissions`. The guard check calls `get_all_permissions`. Thus a user model with only the sync method stopped startup with no cause. A user model with only the async method passed startup, and each request then failed. Now startup checks `get_all_permissions`, and the error names it.
- **The write actions of a ViewSet do not take the primary key from the request body** - `PUT` and `PATCH` of `UpdateMixin` and `PartialUpdateMixin` wrote each serializer field to the object, also the primary key. Thus `PATCH /items/1` with `{"id": 2}` wrote the data of row 1 to row 2, also when row 2 was not in `get_queryset()`. An `id` that no row had inserted a row. `field(read_only=True)` did not prevent this. `POST` with the `id` of a row updated that row. Now `update` and `partial_update` do not write the primary key, the lookup field or a `read_only` field. `create` does not write a `read_only` field, and does not write the primary key when the database gives it. `Serializer.to_model()` and `Serializer.update_instance()` do not write a `read_only` field.
- **A parameter model that forbids unknown fields ignores the keys of other parameters** - A `Query()`, `Cookie()` or `Form()` model with `forbid_unknown_fields=True` got the complete query, cookie or form map. Thus the key of another parameter of the handler, also of a `File()` parameter, gave a 422 (`Object contains unknown field`), and the error had the `loc` `["body"]` for each source. Now the model does not get the keys that the other parameters of the handler declare. A key that no parameter declares gives a 422 with the type `unknown_field` and a `loc` that names the source and the key, for example `["query", "other"]`.
- **A bad `@cors` option stops startup** - `@cors` did not check `methods`, `headers`, `credentials` and `max_age`, and Rust did not read a value of the wrong type. The route got the default of the option, with no error. For example, `@cors(origins=[...], methods="GET")` allowed each default method, and `max_age="600"` gave `3600`. Now `@cors` raises `TypeError` for `methods` or `headers` that is not a list of str, a `credentials` that is not a bool, or a `max_age` that is not an int. A `str` for `methods` or `headers` is an error. It raises `ValueError` for a `max_age` below 0 or above 4294967295. A `cors` dict in the `middleware` of an API or a router gets the same check in Rust at startup, also for `expose_headers`. A bad value raises `TypeError`.
- **A response status that is not an HTTP status is a 500** - Rust sent 200 OK for a status that it could not read, such as `HTTPException(99)`, `JSON(..., status_code=1000)` or status 0 from an ASGI mount. A client then took an error for a success. Now such a status gives a 500, and the Rust log names the bad status. An ASGI mount gets a `ValueError` from `send` for a status that is not from 100 to 999. For a handler and for `status_code=` of a route, the entry "A response status is from 200 to 599" gives the range.
- **An optional parameter model takes its default** - A `Query()`, `Header()`, `Cookie()` or `Form()` model with a default, such as `filters: Annotated[Filters | None, Query()] = None`, never got the default. A request with none of its keys gave a 422 for each required field. Now such a request gives the default, or `None` for an `Optional` type without default. A request with one of the keys of the model validates the model as before.
- **A Python middleware can answer with a response object** - A middleware that returned `JSON(..., status_code=403)`, another response object or a dict, and did not call `get_response`, gave a 500: "Middleware chain returned unsupported response type". Only `raise HTTPException`, a wire tuple and `MiddlewareResponse` worked, although the docstrings promise a returned response. Now a middleware can return what a handler can return. Bolt serializes the value as it serializes the return of the handler of the route, with the headers and the cookies of a response object. A dict gets the status code of the route, and a `response_model` of the route validates it. An outer middleware gets the object that the inner middleware returned. A chain that returns the response of the handler does no more work than before.
- **`@api.viewset(auth=..., guards=...)` protects the `@action` routes** - The `auth` and `guards` arguments of `@api.viewset()` applied only to the standard actions. An `@action` route of the same ViewSet answered with no authentication. Now each `@action` route gets the `auth`, `guards`, `status_code`, `validate_response` and `tags` arguments of the decorator. An `@action` that sets one of these options keeps its value. The class attributes `auth` and `guards` applied to `@action` routes before and still do.
- **`dump(exclude_defaults=True)` drops a field with an empty list or dict default** - msgspec keeps a default such as `tags: list[str] = []` or `msgspec.field(default_factory=list)` as a factory. `dump(exclude_defaults=True)` compared the value with the factory object, so it kept the field at its default. A default from `field(default_factory=list)` did not have this problem. Now `dump()` compares the value with the result of the factory.
- **A ViewSet lookup value of the wrong type answers 404** - `GET`, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /articles/abc` on a ViewSet with an integer primary key answered 500: `ValueError: Field 'id' expected a number but got 'abc'`. `get_object()` caught only `ObjectDoesNotExist`. Now a value that the lookup field cannot hold answers 404 `{"detail": "Not found"}`, as a value that no row has.
- **An error handler returns its own headers list** - `handle_exception`, `http_exception_handler` and `format_error_response` returned one list that each default error response of the process shared. A caller that added a header to the result, such as `headers.append(("x-request-id", "abc"))`, added it to each later error response. Now each call returns a new list.
- **`@middleware` rejects a form that nothing reads** - The docstring of `middleware()` showed `@middleware(cors={"origins": [...]})` and `@middleware(skip=["auth"])`. Both attached a dict that no middleware reads, so the route had no CORS headers and skipped nothing, with no error. A dict config with no `type`, or with an unknown type such as `{"type": "bogus"}`, had the same result in `@middleware` and in the `middleware` list of an API or a router. Now a keyword argument of `@middleware` without `type` raises `TypeError`, and the message names `@cors(...)`, `@skip_middleware(...)` or `@rate_limit(...)`. A dict config whose `type` is not `"cors"` or `"rate_limit"` raises `ValueError` at each level. The docstring shows only the forms that work.
- **An `HTTPException` below 400 does not go to the `error_formatter`** - With an `error_formatter`, each `HTTPException` with a status below 400, such as `HTTPException(307, headers={"Location": "/new"})`, went to the formatter. The formatter can return only a status from 400 to 599, so Bolt refused its result and wrote an ERROR record for each request. The response was correct. Now Bolt sends the default response of such an exception and does not call the formatter.
- **The write actions of a ViewSet answer 422 for a body that is not a JSON object** - `POST`, `PUT` and `PATCH` of the ViewSet mixins answered 500 for malformed JSON and for an empty body (`msgspec.DecodeError`). `PATCH` also answered 500 for a JSON array, string or `null` (`AttributeError`). Now each of these bodies gives the 422 that a function route with the same serializer gives, for example `json_invalid` with the line and the column.
- **A view method gets `self.request` for each position of its request parameter** - The handler of a class-based view took its first argument as the request. Thus `async def get(self)` answered 500 with `IndexError: tuple index out of range`, although the docs show this form. With `async def get(self, user_id: int, request: Request)`, `self.request` was the integer. Now registration finds the parameter with the name `request` or `req`. A method with no such parameter still gets the request for `self.request`. The same applies to `@action` methods.
- **`@skip_middleware` takes a middleware class, and a name ignores the letter case** - `@skip_middleware(SomeMiddleware)`, with the class and not its name, skipped nothing and gave no error. The class in the list also made Rust drop each other name, so `@skip_middleware(SomeMiddleware, "cors")` did not skip CORS. The name of a Python middleware had to match the letter case of the class, but `"CORS"` matched the Rust middleware, and `"RATE_LIMIT"` did not skip the rate limit. Now a class selects that middleware by its dotted path. A name of a Python middleware, a Django middleware or a Rust middleware ignores the letter case. A value that is not a `str` or a class raises `TypeError`.
- **`create_serializer()` keeps the order of the fields** - `create_serializer()` put the field names in a `set`. Thus the field order of the serializer, the order of the JSON keys and the schema changed from one process to the next, also between the workers of one server. Now the fields have the order of the `fields` argument, or the order of the model fields when there is no `fields` argument.
- **`create_serializer()` gives `bool | None` for a `BooleanField(null=True)`** - The type of a null `BooleanField` was `bool`. Thus a serializer from `create_serializer()` rejected `null` for a field that the model accepts. Now each null field has `None` in its type. The docstring of `create_serializer_set()` said that the Update serializer accepts partial input. It now says that each field of `update_fields` is required, which is what the serializer does.
- **A validator error of a renamed serializer names the field as the client sent it** - For a serializer with `rename=`, the `loc` of a `@field_validator` error had the Python name of the field, such as `["body", "email_address"]`. A type error or a missing field of the same body had the wire name, `["body", "emailAddress"]`. Now each error of a field has the wire name. This includes the errors of a nested serializer field and a `Literal` field.
- **A number as a header value of an `HTTPException` keeps the status** - `TooManyRequests(headers={"Retry-After": 30})` gave a 500, because Rust accepts only a `str` value. With an `error_formatter`, the log blamed the formatter. Now `HTTPException` converts an `int` or `float` value to a `str`. A header name or value of another type raises `TypeError` where the application makes the exception. Also, Bolt did not send a custom header that is not valid in HTTP, such as a value with a line break, and wrote no log. Now the Rust core writes a WARNING record with the name of the header.
- **A second `@rate_limit` on one route raises** - With two `@rate_limit` on one function, the route used the limit of the bottom decorator and dropped the other, with no error. For example, `@rate_limit(rps=1, burst=1)` over `@rate_limit(rps=100)` allowed 100 requests per second. A second `@cors` already raised. Now a second `@rate_limit`, or a `@rate_limit` together with `@middleware(type="rate_limit")`, raises `ValueError`.
- **`@api.viewset(lookup_field=...)` reaches `get_object()`** - The `lookup_field` argument of `@api.viewset()` only named the path parameter. `get_object()` read the class attribute, so `GET /articles/t1` with `lookup_field="title"` answered 400 `Missing lookup field: pk`. Now the view instances of the registration get the value of the decorator. The class attribute does not change, so one class can have two registrations with different lookup fields.
- **`PATCH` of a ViewSet works with a renamed serializer** - With `class S(Serializer, rename="camel")`, `partial_update` validated the merged data by field name, but validation reads the wire name. Thus each `PATCH` answered 422 "Object missing required field `isPublished`" when a renamed field was required. When the renamed field had a default, `PATCH {"isPublished": true}` answered 200 and saved the default. Now the merged data uses the wire names.
- **A `get_serializer_class()` override can read `self.action` at registration** - `@api.viewset()` calls `get_serializer_class()` to find the response type of a standard action. `self.action` had no value then, so an override such as `return A if self.action == "list" else B` stopped registration with `LookupError: <ContextVar name='_current_action'>`. Now `self.action` has the action name during this call: "list" for the list route and "retrieve" for the other routes.
- **A response validation error of a Struct has the field in its `loc`** - With `DEBUG=True`, a handler with a Struct response type that returned `{"id": "x"}` got the error `{"loc": ["response"], "msg": "Response validation failed for Item: Expected `int`, got `str` - at `$.id`"}`. The path of the field was in the message. Now the `loc` is `["response", "id"]`, as for a `list[Struct]` and a Serializer, and the message has no path. A missing field has the type `missing_field`.
- **The rate limit of the route wins over the limit of the API** - A route can get a `rate_limit` config from the `middleware` of the API, from its router and from `@rate_limit`. Rust used the first config, which is the config of the API. Thus `@rate_limit(rps=1, burst=1)` on a route had no effect when the API had `{"type": "rate_limit", "rps": 100}`, with no error. Now the most specific config applies: the route, then the router, then the API, as for `cors`. Rust also checks each config of the route at startup. Before, it did not read the configs after the first one.
- **`Serializer.subset()` keeps the struct options of the parent** - A subset of a serializer with `rename="camel"` read and wrote the Python field names, such as `user_name`, while the parent used `userName`. The subset also lost `omit_defaults`, `forbid_unknown_fields` and `frozen`. Now the subset has the wire names and these options of the parent. The subset still does not have the tag or the model validators of the parent, and the docstring says so.
- **A required field with `field()` has the error of a required field** - A field such as `title: str = field(source="name")` has no default, but msgspec got the `field()` marker as its default. Thus a body with no `title` passed msgspec, and the serializer then raised its own error: a 422 with `loc: ["body"]` and the type `validation_error`. The OpenAPI schema also showed the field as optional. Now such a field is a required field of the struct. A missing value gives the error of msgspec, with `loc: ["body", "title"]` and the type `missing_field`. A direct call with no value raises `TypeError`, as for each other required field.
- **Input does not set a `read_only` serializer field** - A request body with `{"id": 99}` set `id: int = field(read_only=True, default=0)` to 99. `model_validate()`, `model_validate_json()` and a nested serializer did the same. Now Bolt ignores the input value, and the field gets its default. A serializer with `forbid_unknown_fields=True` rejects the value, as it rejects an unknown field. Direct construction and `from_model()` still set the field. A `read_only` field that input can reach must have a default. Without one, route registration and `model_validate()` raise `ImproperlyConfigured`. The OpenAPI request body schema of such a serializer has no `read_only` field, and the response schema marks the field `readOnly: true`. If an API uses the serializer for the two, the request body uses the component `<Name>Input`.
- **`Serializer.model_validate()` lists each value that the conversion rejects** - After a failed conversion, `model_validate()` and `model_validate_json()` checked each field again, but with the lax mode of msgspec. Thus `{"age": "12"}` for an `int` field was not in the error list, and with no other error the call raised a plain `msgspec.ValidationError`. The `loc` of an error in a list item had the form `("body", "items", "items[1]", "city")`. Now the check of each field is strict, as the conversion is. The `loc` has one item for each step, `("body", "items", "1", "city")`, as a missing field has. A nested serializer whose validators fail gives one entry for each error.
- **The `create` action of a ViewSet only inserts** - `perform_create()` called `asave()`. With a primary key that the client supplies, `POST` with the key of an existing row updated that row and answered 201. Now `perform_create()` saves with `force_insert=True`. A primary key or a unique value that a row already has answers 409 `{"detail": "The object conflicts with an existing object"}`, and the row does not change. An override of `perform_create()` that calls `obj.asave()` directly keeps the old save.
- **`@rate_limit` refuses a `key` that is not a header name** - A `key` other than `"ip"`, `"user"` and `"api_key"` is the name of a request header. `@rate_limit` accepted each non-empty str, for example `key=" "` or a key with a line break. No request has such a header, so all callers of the route shared one bucket, with no error. Now `@rate_limit` raises `ValueError` for a `key` with a character that a header name cannot have, such as a space, a colon or a line break.
- **The OpenAPI document shows the media type of a response class in the return annotation** - A route with `-> StreamingResponse`, `-> FileResponse` or another response class as its return annotation showed `application/json` with `{"type": "object"}`. A `Redirect` annotation showed a 200 with a JSON body. With `response_class=` and the same annotation, the media type was correct but the schema was `{"type": "object"}`. Now the annotation gives the same response as `response_class=`.
- **`runbolt` stops for an option value out of range** - `--port 65536` and `--workers 513` failed after startup with a traceback. `--processes 0` with a recycling option started no worker and exited with status 0. Negative `--max-rss` and `--workers-lifetime` values recycled a worker each second. `runbolt` now prints an error that names the option and its limit, and exits with a non-zero status.
- **A generator dependency and a `*args` or `**kwargs` parameter stop the route registration** - Bolt accepted a generator function as a dependency. The handler got the generator object, and the code after `yield` did not run. Bolt also accepted `*args` and `**kwargs` on a dependency or a handler, and then each request got a 422 `Missing required query parameter: args`. Now Bolt raises `TypeError` for these shapes when it registers the route.
- **A middleware class needs callable instances** - `BoltAPI(middleware=[int])` registered. Then each request answered 500, because Bolt calls the instance of the class with the request. Now the `middleware` of an API, a router and a route raises `TypeError` for a class with no `__call__` method. A plain class with `__init__(get_response)` and `__call__(request)` is still a middleware, with or without a base class of Bolt.
- **The OpenAPI document shows `bytes` in JSON as base64 text** - A `bytes`, `bytearray` or `memoryview` value in a JSON body showed `{"type": "string", "format": "binary"}`. msgspec writes and reads these types in JSON as base64 text, so a generated client sent raw bytes that the server rejects. Now the schema is `{"type": "string", "contentEncoding": "base64"}`, the OpenAPI 3.1 form. A `File()` parameter keeps `format: binary`.
- **`ConnectionClosed` of `WebSocketTestClient` has the close reason** - `receive_text()` and `receive_bytes()` raised `ConnectionClosed` with an empty `reason` for each close. The error now has the reason that the handler gave to `close()`.
- **The OpenAPI document shows only `multipart/form-data` for a route with a file** - The request body of a route with a `File()` parameter, or with a form struct that has an `UploadFile` field, also showed `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` with the file as `format: binary`. A urlencoded body cannot hold a file, and the server answers 422 for it. Now such a route shows `multipart/form-data` only. A route with only form fields keeps both content types.
- **A CORS value that Rust cannot use stops startup** - Bolt dropped an origin pattern that did not compile and wrote only a warning on stderr. It dropped the `Access-Control-Allow-Methods`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` or `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` header when a name had a character that is not valid in an HTTP header, such as a line break. Now `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_REGEXES`, `CORS_ALLOW_METHODS`, `CORS_ALLOW_HEADERS` and `CORS_EXPOSE_HEADERS` raise `ImproperlyConfigured` at startup. `@cors` and a `cors` dict raise `ValueError` at startup. The error names the setting or the option, and the value. A pattern with look-around stopped no server before. It stops startup now, because the Rust `regex` crate cannot compile it.
- **`api.viewset()` registers a sync standard action** - A `ViewSet` with `def list`, `def retrieve` or another standard action as a sync method got no route for it, with no error. The client got 404. A sync `APIView` method and a sync `@action` already worked. Now a sync standard action gets its route.
- **`mode=` of `@field_validator` and `@model_validator` has an effect** - The decorators stored `mode` and nothing read it. Validators ran in definition order, and a `mode="before"` validator got the converted value. Now a field validator with `mode="before"` gets the raw input value before the type conversion, and the type of the field validates its result. A model validator with `mode="before"` is a classmethod that gets the raw input data and returns the data to validate. Before validators run before after validators, in the order of pydantic. `mode="wrap"`, `mode="plain"` and each other value raise `ValueError` when the class is created. A serializer with no before validator keeps the direct msgspec decode.
- **The OpenAPI document shows the media type of a `str` or `bytes` return type** - A route with `-> str` sends `text/plain`, and a route with `-> bytes` sends `application/octet-stream`. The document showed `application/json` for both. Now it shows `text/plain` with a string schema and `application/octet-stream` with a binary string schema.
- **The default logging setup prints a `django` record one time** - With no `LOGGING` setting, `runbolt` printed each record of the `django` logger and of its children two times, for example the `django.request` line of a 500. The `django` logger had the console handler and also sent each record to the root logger. It now prints each record one time.
- **`Config.validators` of a `Serializer` is an error** - The base `Config` declared `validators`, and nothing read it. A serializer that set it had no validation from it and gave no error. Now a `Config` with `validators` raises `TypeError` when the class is created. The message names `@field_validator` and `@model_validator`. The base `Config` has no `validators` attribute.
- **A `cors` or `rate_limit` dict gets the checks of its decorator** - `@middleware(type="cors", ...)` did not get the Python checks of `@cors`. For example, it took a config with no origins. A `rate_limit` dict of an API or a router took a `key` that is not a header name. Now `@cors` and `@rate_limit` share their checks with the dict form. A dict in `@middleware`, or in the `middleware` of an API or a router, raises the error of the decorator when you register it. Before, Rust raised `TypeError` for a wrong type when the server started. The `cors` config of a route needs an origin or a pattern. The `cors` dict of an API or a router does not, because it can keep the allow list of the outer config.
- **A parameter model does not set a `read_only` serializer field** - A `Serializer` as a `Query()`, `Header()`, `Cookie()` or `Form()` model took a `read_only` field from the request: `?id=99` set `id: int = field(read_only=True, default=0)` to 99. Now these models follow the rule of the JSON body. Bolt ignores the value, and the field gets its default. A serializer with `forbid_unknown_fields=True` rejects the value, as it rejects an unknown field.
- **The admin mount uses the prefix of the admin site** - With `re_path(r"^manage/", admin.site.urls)`, `runbolt` mounted the admin at `/^manage`, and `/manage/` answered 404. When a pattern before the admin site had "admin" in the name of its module, `runbolt` mounted the admin at the prefix of that pattern. `runbolt` now finds the pattern of the admin site first and removes the regex anchors from its prefix.
- **`exclude_paths` of `OpenAPIConfig` matches whole path segments** - The generator compared the start of the path text. With the default `exclude_paths=["/admin", "/static"]`, a route such as `/administrators` or `/static-pages` was not in the OpenAPI document. A route such as `/docs-archive` was also not in the document, because its path starts with the docs path. Now `/admin` excludes `/admin` and each path below `/admin/`.
- **`nanodjango convert` keeps class views, routers and mount calls** - The convert hook moved only `BoltAPI()` assignments, decorated functions, and `mount()` and `mount_django()` calls to `api.py`. A class with `@bolt.view()` or `@bolt.viewset()` and a `Router` with its routes went to `unused.py`. The calls `bolt.include_router()`, `bolt.mount_asgi()`, `bolt.mount_mcp()` and `bolt.native_route()` were not in the generated project at all. The hook now moves each of them to `api.py`.
- **A subclass replaces a validator that it defines again** - A subclass of a `Serializer` that defined a `@field_validator` or `@model_validator` method again under the same name ran the validator of the parent and its own. Now only the definition of the subclass runs, at the position of the parent definition. This is the rule of pydantic. A validator with a new name still adds to those of the parent. A new definition with no validator decorator removes the validator.
- **The OpenAPI schema keeps the null of `T | None`** - A response type such as `int | None` or `list[int | None]` gave the schema of `int`. The JSON body type `Item | None` gave the schema of `Item`, but Bolt accepts the body `null`. Now such a type gives `anyOf` with a `{"type": "null"}` arm, as the same type gives as a struct field. An optional path, query, header, cookie or form parameter does not change: `required: false` says that it is optional. A `T | None` item type in a list or dict parameter now also has the null arm.
- **The docs pages escape the text of the API** - The Swagger UI and ReDoc pages hold the OpenAPI document as JSON in a `<script>` element. A docstring or a description with `</script>` ended that script, and the page did not load. The API title went into `<title>` as HTML. An apostrophe in the `options` of `ScalarRenderPlugin` ended a JavaScript string. Now the JSON in a script has no `<`, `>` or `&`, and the title is escaped.
- **An empty pattern in `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_REGEXES` stops startup** - Bolt accepted an empty pattern in the setting, for example `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGIN_REGEXES = [""]`. An empty pattern matches every origin. Thus each origin got `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` with `CORS_ALLOW_CREDENTIALS = True`. `@cors` and a `cors` dict already rejected an empty pattern. Now the setting raises `ImproperlyConfigured` at startup.
- **A page of a plain `msgspec.Struct` type validates its items** - `@paginate` on a handler with the return type `list[Item]`, where `Item` is a plain `msgspec.Struct`, read each field with a default of `None`. Thus an object with no such attribute gave `null` for a required field, with status 200. The paginator also took the kind of all items from the first item, so a dict after a Struct gave `null` for each field. Now Bolt converts the page as it converts a `list[Item]` response type with no `@paginate`. The page has the same items: the defaults of the Struct and its wire names (`rename=`). A value that does not fit gives the same response validation error (status 500). `build_response()` gets instances of the Struct, not dicts. A Bolt `Serializer` item type does not change.
- **A WebSocket route takes an async dependency** - A WebSocket handler with an `async def` dependency got a 500 at the handshake under `runbolt`. The handshake called the injector of the route with no await. `WebSocketTestClient` awaited the injector, so a test passed where the server failed. The route now awaits the injector in the handler coroutine, and `WebSocketTestClient` calls the injector as the server does.
- **A `cors` or `rate_limit` dict refuses a key that nothing reads** - Bolt ignored an unknown key in a `cors` or `rate_limit` dict, with no error. For example, `BoltAPI(middleware=[{"type": "cors", "origin": ["https://a.test"]}])` started, and the origin list that the author meant did not apply. Now such a key raises `TypeError` when you register the dict, and the error lists the valid keys. This applies to a dict in the `middleware` of an API or a router and to `@middleware(type="cors", ...)`. The decorators `@cors(...)` and `@rate_limit(...)` already refused an unknown keyword argument.
- **A handler gets the disconnect when `WebSocketTestClient` exits** - The exit of the `async with` block cancelled the handler at once. The handler did not get the disconnect, so the code after its receive loop and its `except WebSocketDisconnect` block did not run in a test. On the server they run. The client now waits up to 1 second for the handler to end, and then cancels it. A `WebSocketDisconnect` that the handler does not catch is not a handler error, as on the server.
- **A WebSocket route accepts a connection with and without a trailing slash** - A WebSocket route accepts a connection with and without a trailing slash in the request path, in each `trailing_slash` mode. Before, a route declared with a trailing slash accepted no connection, and the server refused a request path with a trailing slash that `WebSocketTestClient` accepted. A WebSocket route path now has no trailing slash in the OpenAPI schema and in `reverse()`.
- **The OpenAPI schema shows the 422 of a route with parameters** - The schema showed a 422 response only for a route with a request body. A route with only path, query, header or cookie parameters showed no 422, but a bad value gives 422. Each route with a parameter or a request body now shows its 422. For a route with a parameter or a form field, `detail` in the 422 schema is a list of field errors or a text, because Bolt sends the two forms. A route with no parameter and no body shows no 422. `OpenAPIConfig(strict=True)` now also reports a formatter without `error_schema` when only such a route has a 422.
- **A handler can return a number, a `bool`, a date, a `UUID`, a `Decimal` or an `Enum` member** - A handler that returned `3` got a 500 with "Handler returned unsupported type 'int'", also with `-> int`, and OpenAPI showed an integer response. Now Bolt sends such a scalar as JSON (`3`, `true`, `"2020-01-02"`), with or without a response type. Bolt sends `None` as `null` when the response type allows it (`-> None`, `-> int | None`). With no such type, `None` stays an error, because the handler has no `return`. An `int`, `float` or `bool` response type is now validated as each other type: `-> int` with a `dict` result is a response validation error.
- **The OpenAPI schema shows the security of a WebSocket route that uses `BOLT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES`** - A WebSocket route with no `auth` argument uses the backends of `BOLT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES`, and the server applies them. But the operation of the route had no `security` entry in the schema. An HTTP route with the same backends had one. Now the WebSocket operation lists the same security schemes as an HTTP route.
- **A second handler for the method and path of a route stops startup** - Two handlers with the same method and static path, such as `@api.get("/d")` two times, registered with no error, and the last handler answered. A path with a parameter raised `ValueError`. Two mounted APIs with the same route had the same fault. Now each such route raises `ValueError` at startup. The OpenAPI docs routes register each path one time.
- **A route path with a space or a non-ASCII character matches its requests** - A client sends such a character as a `%XX` escape, and the router compared the request path with the route path as it was written. Thus `@api.get("/ü")`, `@api.get("/a b")` and `@api.get("/ü/{x}")` answered 404 for each request. Now the router stores the route path with the escapes of a URL path. A path parameter and a `%` stay as they are.
- **`mount()` applies the `prefix` of the parent API** - `BoltAPI(prefix="/api").mount("/v1", child)` served the routes of `child` at `/v1/...`. The prefix applied to each route, native route and ASGI mount of the parent, but not to a mounted API. Now the mounted routes, WebSocket routes, native routes, ASGI mounts and MCP mounts of `child` are at `/api/v1/...`. An application that used the old paths must mount the child on an API with no prefix.
- **A handler from a module that is not in `sys.modules` registers** - Bolt read the globals for the annotations of a handler from `sys.modules[handler.__module__]`. A function made with `exec`, or one from a module that was removed from `sys.modules`, failed at registration with `AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute '__dict__'`. A function with a changed `__module__` failed with `NameError`. Now Bolt uses the globals of the function, as `typing.get_type_hints` does, and follows `__wrapped__`.
- **A path or a prefix with no leading slash gets one** - `BoltAPI(prefix="api")`, `@api.get("x")`, `Router(prefix="users")` and `include_router(router, prefix="v1")` joined the parts as they were. Thus the route was `apix` or `v1users`, and each request got 404. Only the path of `mount()` got its leading slash. Now each route path, WebSocket path, view path, viewset path and prefix gets a leading slash.
- **`Annotated[bytes, File()]` gives the content of the file** - A `bytes` file parameter got the file dict that Rust makes (`filename`, `content_type`, `size`, `content`, `temp_path`), and `list[bytes]` got a list of such dicts. Now the handler gets the content of the upload as `bytes`, or a list with the content of each file. This is also true for a file that Rust wrote to disk.
- **The OpenAPI schema shows the 401 and the 403 of a guarded route, and the right security scheme** - A route with an auth backend and a guard showed only its success response, but a request with no credential gets 401. Such a route now shows a 401 with the `WWW-Authenticate` header for `IsAuthenticated` or `Requires`, and a 403 for `Requires`. A route with `SessionAuthentication(login_url=...)` shows the 302 to the login page in place of the 401. The body is `{"detail": "<message>"}` or the body of the `error_formatter`. A route with no auth backend, with no guard, or with `AllowAny` first shows none of them. The security scheme of `JWTAuthentication(cookie=...)` and of `JWTAuthentication(header=...)` was `http` `bearer`, but Bolt reads the token from the cookie or from that header. These backends now get an `apiKey` scheme, `JWTCookieAuth` or `JWTHeaderAuth`. Two `APIKeyAuthentication` backends with different headers shared the scheme of the first header. Each header now has its own scheme.
- **The OpenAPI example of a tagged union keeps a `null` field** - The schema shows one example for each variant of a tagged `Struct` union. A field of the type `None` was not in the example, but the response body has it with the value `null`. The example now has each field of the variant.
- **A `bytes` parameter gets the UTF-8 bytes of its text value** - A `bytes` query, path, header, cookie or form parameter gave the handler a `str`, with no conversion and no error. `list[bytes]` and `bytes` with a `msgspec.Meta` constraint read the text as base64. Now each of them gets the UTF-8 bytes of the text, and `bytearray` does too. A `bytes` field of a parameter model still reads base64, as msgspec does.
- **A config decorator above the route decorator stops startup** - `@rate_limit`, `@cors`, `@middleware`, `@skip_middleware`, `@no_compress` or `@paginate` above `@api.get(...)` had no effect and gave no error. The route decorator had read the configuration of the handler before the upper decorator ran. Now `runbolt` and `TestClient` raise `TypeError` at startup, and the message says to put the decorator below the route decorator. A decorator above `@router.get(...)` applies, as before.
- **An object with `__call__` is a route handler, and a `functools.partial` gets a clear error** - `api.get("/p")(handler)` raised `AttributeError: ... has no attribute '__name__'` for a callable object and for a `functools.partial`. A bound method worked. Now the route registers the bound `__call__` method of a callable object. A `functools.partial` raises `TypeError` with the cause: registration cannot read its type hints, and its keyword arguments would become parameters that a request can set.
- **`mount()` refuses an API that it cannot serve** - `api.mount("/s", api)` never returned, because the loop added routes to the list that it read. And with `api.mount("/t/{x}", child)`, a handler of `child` with the argument `x` answered 422 "Missing required query parameter: x", because the mounted route was compiled without the mount path. Now `mount()` raises `ValueError` for the API itself, for an API that already has the routes of this API, and for a handler that takes a mount path parameter as an argument. A mounted handler reads a mount path parameter from `request.params`, as before.
- **A path with one parameter name two times stops registration** - `@api.get("/d/{x}/{x}")` registered, and the handler got the last value: `/d/1/2` gave `x` as 2. Now the route decorators, `native_route()` and `mount()` raise `ValueError` for a path that has one parameter name two times, also when the prefix or the mount path has the first one.
- **`BoltAPI(trailing_slash=...)` refuses an unknown mode** - A value other than `"strip"`, `"append"` or `"keep"`, such as `"bogus"` or `"Strip"`, was accepted and behaved as `"keep"`. Now `BoltAPI()` raises `ValueError` for it.
- **`api.viewset()` with a trailing slash in its path, and `api.view()` with an empty `methods` list** - `api.viewset("/items/")` gave the detail route `/items//{pk}`, which no request matches, and the same double slash for each `@action` route. Now the parts join with one slash. With `trailing_slash="keep"`, each route keeps the trailing slash of the viewset path. `api.view("/x", methods=[])` registered no route and gave no error. Now it raises `ValueError`.
- **A response type validates a `str` or `bytes` result** - With `-> int`, `-> Decimal` or a Struct as the response type, a `str` result was sent as `text/plain` with status 200, and a `bytes` result as `application/octet-stream`. The check for a `str` ran before the validation. Now such a result is validated as a `dict` result is, and a wrong value is a response validation error. A response type that names `str` or `bytes` allows the result with no check, such as `-> str`, `-> str | None` or `-> bytes | Item`. A `str` that passes the validation as a `str` stays `text/plain`, such as a `Literal` member. A `str` that the type converts, such as a `UUID` in text, is now JSON. A route with `validate_response=False` does not change.
- **The OpenAPI document has the request body of a body that is not a struct** - A route with `items: Annotated[list[Item], Body()]` reads the JSON body, but its operation had no `requestBody`. The generator read only a struct body. Now each parameter that reads the body gives a `requestBody` with the schema of its type, and `required` is false when the parameter has a default.
- **An HTTP ASGI mount gets the percent-decoded path** - The scope of a `mount_asgi()` or `mount_django()` app had the request bytes in `path`, for example `/caf%C3%A9/a%20b`. The ASGI specification says that `path` is percent-decoded, and Django matches its URL patterns with it. Thus a mounted Django app answered 404 for a URL with an encoded character, such as a space or a letter that is not ASCII. Now `path` is decoded (`/café/a b`), as in the scope of a WebSocket mount. `raw_path` keeps the request bytes.
- **The `WWW-Authenticate` header of a 401 names the scheme of the auth backends of the route** - Each 401 from Rust had `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`, also on a route with only an API key backend or only a session backend. A 401 from Python, such as an `HTTPException(401)` of a handler, had no challenge. Now Bolt builds the header value one time at registration, from the backends of the route in their order. `JWTAuthentication` with the token in the `Authorization` header gives `Bearer`. A JWT in another header or in a cookie, `APIKeyAuthentication` and `SessionAuthentication` give `ApiKey`. A route with a JWT backend and an API key backend sends `Bearer, ApiKey`. A WebSocket handshake and an MCP mount send the challenge of their backends. Rust adds the header to each 401 of the route that has none, and a header that your code sets stays. A backend subclass can override `challenge_scheme`. The status and the body of the 401 do not change.
- **The scope of `request_started` has the query string and the headers** - With `BOLT_EMIT_SIGNALS = True`, the scope always had `query_string` as `b""`, because the code read a request key that does not exist. `headers` was `{}` unless the route read a header. Now Bolt keeps the query string and the headers of each request when signals are on, and the scope has both. Nothing changes when signals are off.
- **A `dict` parameter with no marker reads the JSON body** - On a `POST`, `PUT` or `PATCH` route, Bolt read a parameter such as `data: dict` or `counts: dict[str, int]` from the query string. A query string cannot hold a dict. Thus a JSON body got 422 with `Missing required query parameter`, and `?data=x` gave the handler a `str`. Now such a parameter is the JSON body, as a Struct is, and a `dict[K, V]` type validates it. On a route with another method, a `dict` parameter stops the registration (see "A parameter type that Bolt cannot convert stops the registration"). OpenAPI now shows the request body of a JSON body that is not a Struct, also with the `Body()` marker.
- **`render()` sends `text/html; charset=utf-8`** - `render()` of `django_bolt.shortcuts` sent `content-type: text/html` with no charset. `HTML()` and `render()` of Django send the charset. Now the default content type of `render()` is `text/html; charset=utf-8`. Bolt still sends a `content_type` that you pass with no change.
- **`close()` of a WebSocket refuses a code or a reason that makes an invalid close frame** - `await websocket.close(reason="x" * 200)` sent a close frame with a payload of 202 bytes. RFC 6455 limits it to 125 bytes: 2 bytes of code and 123 bytes of reason. A browser fails such a connection. A code above 65535 raised `OverflowError` from Rust, and `WebSocketTestClient` accepted it. Now `close()` raises `ValueError` for a reason above 123 bytes of UTF-8, and for a code that is not from 1000 to 4999 or that is 1005, 1006 or 1015. The connection stays open after the error. The server does the same check on the `websocket.close` message of a mounted ASGI app, and `WebSocketTestClient` raises the same errors.
- **The OpenAPI document shows the media type of a `str` or `bytes` arm of a union and of a `response_model` entry** - A route sends a `str` result as `text/plain` and a `bytes` result as `application/octet-stream`. The document showed only `application/json` for `-> str | None`, `-> bytes | None`, `-> str | Item` and for a `str` or `bytes` entry of a `response_model` dict, such as `{200: str, 404: Error}` or `{404: Error, ...: str}`. Now the document shows `text/plain` for a `str` arm, `application/octet-stream` for a `bytes` arm, and `application/json` for the other arms. For `-> str | None`, the `application/json` schema is `null`.
- **A request path matches its route for each form of a `%XX` escape** - The router compared the request path byte for byte. Thus `GET /%c3%bc` got 404 where `GET /%C3%BC` matched `@api.get("/ü")`, and `GET /%61` got 404 for the route `/a`. Now the router reads an escape in lower case as the escape in upper case (RFC 3986, section 6.2.2.1). It reads an escape of a character that a path holds with no escape, such as `%61` or `%3A`, as that character, as Django does. `%2F` and `%25` stay escapes: an escaped `/` does not split a segment. A `%` that starts no escape is a literal `%`, so the route `/100%` matches `GET /100%25`. A route path gets the same form.
- **A subclass of `Decimal`, `datetime` or `time` encodes as its base type** - msgspec encodes the exact types itself, and a subclass went to an encoder of Bolt that gave a different result. A subclass of `Decimal` became a JSON number (`1.5`), while a `Decimal` is a string (`"1.50"`). A subclass that held `NaN` or `Infinity` raised `TypeError`, and the client got a 500. A subclass of `datetime` or `time` in UTC ended with `+00:00`, while the base type ends with `Z`. Now the encoder gives msgspec a value of the exact base type, so the JSON is the same. **Behavior change:** code that relied on the number of a `Decimal` subclass now gets a string.
- **A WebSocket route path with a space or a non-ASCII character accepts connections** - A client sends such a character as a `%XX` escape, and the WebSocket router compared the request path with the route path as it was written. Thus `@api.websocket("/ü")` and `@api.websocket("/a b/{room}")` refused each handshake with 404, and `WebSocketTestClient` raised `No WebSocket handler found for path`. Now the WebSocket router stores the route path with the escapes of a URL path, as the HTTP router does.
- **The admin mount finds an admin site in a nested `include()` or at the root path** - `runbolt` read only the top level of the URLconf. An admin site in a nested `include()` got the mount `/admin`, and its real path answered 404. Now `runbolt` takes the prefix from `reverse("admin:index")`, so Django resolves it through the URL resolver tree. With `path("", admin.site.urls)`, `runbolt` also mounted Django at `/admin`. Now it mounts Django at `/` and prints a warning: Bolt routes still answer first, and Django answers each path that has no Bolt route. The startup banner shows the same path. An error in the import of `ROOT_URLCONF` now stops `runbolt`. Before, `runbolt` printed a warning and mounted `/admin`.
- **`@api.websocket()` takes an object with an async `__call__` and a bound method** - `api.websocket("/ws")(handler)` raised `AttributeError: ... has no attribute '__name__'` for a callable object and `AttributeError: 'method' object has no attribute '_is_websocket_handler'` for a bound method. A `functools.partial` raised a `TypeError` from `typing`. Now the route registers a bound method, and the bound `__call__` method of a callable object, as an HTTP route does. A `functools.partial` raises `TypeError` with the cause. The error for a handler that is not `async` names the handler with its qualified name.
- **`runbolt --workers-kill-timeout` works with one process** - With one process and no recycling option, the option had no effect: only the supervising parent passed it to the server. A request that was still running at `SIGTERM` kept the server up for the default 30 seconds. Now the server process itself reads the option, so it is the graceful-shutdown window in each mode. `DJANGO_BOLT_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT` still takes precedence. The option alone does not start the supervising parent. The deployment guide said that it does, and now it says what the option does.
- **A WebSocket handler that returns closes the connection** - A handler that returned after `accept()` with no `close()` left the connection open. The server sent no close frame, and the client waited with no end. Now the server sends a close frame with code 1000 when the handler returns. A mounted ASGI app that returns gets the same close. A handler that raises still gets code 1011 with the reason `Internal error`. `WebSocketTestClient` gives the same close: the receive methods raise `ConnectionClosed` with code 1000 after the last message.
- **The path of a docs UI plugin with no leading slash gets one** - `OpenAPIConfig` gave its own `path` a leading slash, but not the path of a render plugin. Thus `OpenAPIConfig(path="docs", render_plugins=[SwaggerRenderPlugin(path="swagger")])` served the page at `/docsswagger`, and `/docs/swagger` answered 404. Now each plugin path gets a leading slash and loses its trailing slash, as the path of the config does.
- **`WebSocketTestClient` has no `auth_context` option** - The option said that it gave the guards an authentication context. The guards did not read it, so a route with `guards=[IsAuthenticated()]` still answered `Authentication required`. The option is removed, and `WebSocketTestClient(..., auth_context=...)` now raises `TypeError`. To authenticate in a test, send the credential that the server reads, for example `headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"}`.
- **`WebSocketTestClient` gives the close reason of a handler error** - When a handler raises after `accept()`, the server closes with code 1011 and the reason `Internal error`. `WebSocketTestClient` gave code 1011 with an empty reason. `ConnectionClosed` now has the reason `Internal error`, as on the server.
- **A JWT of an inactive user loads no user** - A valid token of a user with `is_active=False` gave that user to `request.user`, `request.auser()` and `CurrentUser`. Session authentication rejects such a user. Now the default user loader returns no user, as Django's `ModelBackend.get_user` does: `request.user` is falsy and `CurrentUser` answers 401. The check reads the loaded row and adds no query. `/auth/refresh` of `JWTAuthViews` with the default credential check now answers 401 for an inactive or deleted user. A route that does not read the user still accepts the token.
- **A wrong method on a route path answers 405 with `Allow`** - A request to a path that has routes, but none for its method, got 404. For example, `POST /x` on an API with only `@api.get("/x")`. Bolt now answers `405 Method Not Allowed`, and the `Allow` header lists the methods of the path (RFC 9110 section 15.5.6). The error formatter gets the kind `method_not_allowed`. A path with no route still gets 404.
- **A raised `WebSocketException` closes the connection with its code and reason** - `WebSocketException` and `WebSocketClose` are public names, but nothing handled them. A handler that raised `WebSocketException(code=4001, reason="nope")` got a close frame with code 1011 and an error in the log. Now the server sends a close frame with the code and the reason of the exception. Before `accept()`, the exception refuses the handshake with 403, as `close()` does. `WebSocketClose` gets the same close. `WebSocketTestClient` gives the same close and the same refusal, and its exit does not raise the exception again.
- **`APIKeyAuthentication` rejects a key that starts with `Bearer ` or `ApiKey `** - Bolt removes one of these scheme words from the value of the API key header. Thus the key `ApiKey xyz` got a 401 when a client sent it as it is. With the keys `abc` and `Bearer abc`, the header value `Bearer abc` authenticated as the key `abc`, with the permissions of that key. Now such a key raises `ImproperlyConfigured` at construction, in any letter case of the scheme. The docs now state the three forms of the header value: `<key>`, `Bearer <key>` and `ApiKey <key>`.
- **An `@action` method can have the name of an HTTP method or of a standard action** - `api.viewset()` did not register an `@action` method with the name `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `list`, `retrieve`, `create`, `update`, `partial_update` or `destroy`. The method got no route and no error, so `/products/options` answered 404. Now each `@action` method is a route, with its path from the `@action` arguments.
- **`receive_text()` and `receive_bytes()` raise for a frame of the other type** - `WebSocket.receive_text()` gave `""` for a binary frame, and `receive_bytes()` gave `b""` for a text frame. The data of the frame was lost with no error. Now each raises `WebSocketException` with code 1003, and the reason names the frame: `receive_text() got a binary frame`. The connection stays open, and the next call reads the next frame. `receive_json()` and the iterators raise the same error. `receive()` does not change.
- **A failed login of `JWTAuthViews` has a `WWW-Authenticate` header** - `POST /auth/login` with wrong credentials answered 401 with no challenge. The login route has no auth backend, so Bolt had no challenge to add. RFC 9110 section 15.5.2 requires one in each 401. The 401 now has the challenge of the token routes of the views.
- **A parameter type that Bolt cannot convert stops the registration** - A path, query, header, cookie or form parameter of a type such as `complex`, `dict` or a dataclass got the text of the request. The handler of `def h(value: complex)` got the str `"abc"` for `/h?value=abc`, with no error. A `File()` parameter of a type such as `str` got the file dict.
